Rule a repair must hold: the application ErrorHandler reports each error it receives to the console with the original error value, as Angular's default handler does, and still dispatches errorEvents.unhandled; a spec under libs/shared/state asserts both the console report of the original error and the dispatch. | The repair lands, or a change touches libs/shared/state/src/lib/dotln-error-handler.ts or the ErrorHandler provider in provideDotlnState(). |

Criterion 4 holds: the spec issues an unmatched request and asserts the failure, and an independent probe shows that the installed 'error' option refuses an unmatched request a live server would answer. The state rules and the events-and-state document say the test proves the option, which it does not yet do. Forces fade to nothing at their edges: arrive's wanted speed reaches nothing halfway inside the target radius, and separation, flee and the anchor's speed fade at theirs. A unit nothing steers brakes. A wall zeroes the velocity into it. Round the objective a unit is heading for, priority's separation group sheds the unit's speed as well as parting it (D016). Formation membership has one record, the assignment, and behaviour.formation and the formation id are gone. A two-level formation that loses members moves its anchor to the centre of the slots still held. Arrival excludes a fixed formation's followers, and D016 gives the rest of it its final form. The stopwatch restarts only when a unit newly heads for the objective, in one place in applyStep. The known outcome was regenerated for this. | The operator's play says a rule feels wrong. One limit is left for that feedback, measured on the final engine: a crowd under priority steering does not fully settle. Over 60 random runs each it keeps a faint shimmer, so it rarely pauses by itself. Priority with Arrive: 55 never pause, peak 0.58 over the last ten seconds, mean 0.053. Priority alone: 55, 0.55, 0.054. Flock, Arrive and Priority together: 58, 0.67, 0.112. Wedge with Priority rests every time. A settled crowd is also slow to start again, so sent somewhere near, it can read as arrived early: 14 of 129 random re-targets were credited with the centre more than 5 off. |

Due steps go before an operator's step on the same frame, and a refusal stays until the operator's own step or a rewind. A trial covers the ten seconds from the split: the prefix runs once and none of it is kept, and Trial gains from. Only a save writes to storage: writeWhen is replaced by writeOn, a save reads storage first so another tab's compositions survive, unless the last save failed and this tab holds the only copy, a failed write is reported through unwritable and a save-failed notice, and one unreadable composition costs only itself. Storage and import share one validator, and what the engine can run is the engine's own isSafeStep: numbers it can square, positive times, and a last step inside eight hours of play. parseExport checks the schema tag and the scene. The slot table is the formation's own once applied, with a running count in the sticky Apply row. A tool on these units opens as it was set. Restart ends liveness. stored-ignored stays in the notice slot, where every other reason sits, rather than in the tool log the Design named. | The operator's play says the ten seconds are the wrong window, or a later scene needs a run longer than the eight-hour bound. |

| WO-015-D016 (`dotln-angular@b4c51461014a835ea470542de3ab5b95a570d86d:docs/dotln/evidence/WO-015/decisions.md#wo-015-d016`) | resume: next; the reviewers' second and third looks at the fix pass, during the close-out D015 authorized | Correct what the fix pass broke, and replace the arrival rule rather than patch it. A group has arrived on one of two routes. It has crossed: its centre is within reach of the objective, its braking units are slow, and every part of it is there, a free unit within the crowd's reach of the objective and a formation's anchor on it. Or it has stopped: every driver stayed slow for half a second, with the centre within the crowd's reach and everyone within twice that. The crowd's reach is the target radius plus 0.8 of the separation threshold times the root of the group's size. Seekers and wanderers need not slow; a formation's members always brake. The wall credit is removed, because the stopped route covers what it was for. Under priority steering the separation group sheds a unit's speed only within 10 of the objective that unit is heading for. A fixed leader and its members keep the formation's own spacing, in blended and priority steering alike. A scene is not at rest while an anchor still moves. A fixed leader's lead on the objective is derived while it leads, for a leader with neither a seek nor an arrive weight, and is never stored. A new composition's id is one past the highest in the list. The step bound moves to eight hours of play, and the anchor lag limit joins the numbers refused below nothing. | The operator's play shows arrival reading wrongly, or a population much larger than twenty changes how far a crowd spreads. |

| WO-022-D024 (`dotln-angular@5f7726370cae58c6ab1f0befdd67ebfc0fe3b117:docs/dotln/evidence/WO-022/decisions.md#wo-022-d024`) | resume: next; step 4, the records that do not run, for criterion 3 | Nine of the twenty records carry pseudocode.executable null, each for the reason its author gave. Request-Reply (eip:83) is a protocol of two one-way channels and a requestor that waits or is called back; a marble run would make the reply a function's return value, which is remote procedure invocation, and its reading is illustrative. Guaranteed Delivery (eip:71) is about durable storage on each machine, crashes and acknowledgements, which an in-memory stream dies with. Control Bus (eip:184) is a layout of channels and ports beside the work, not a transformation of one stream. Message Store (eip:192) is a durable archive queried after the streams end; its reading is illustrative. Transactional Client (eip:170) is about when a receive and its sends become visible across a broker and other stores, a durability boundary. Canonical Data Model (eip:146) is an agreed data model every participant translates to and from, a structure rather than an operation. Channel Adapter (eip:73) attaches a channel to an application that knows no streams, which a marble example could only fake; its reading is illustrative. Command Message (eip:78) is a request for one receiver to act, and its one-taker delivery belongs to the point-to-point channel. Event Message (eip:80) is defined by its meaning and timing, and the delivery and age checks around it belong to other patterns; its reading is illustrative. The other eleven run, each with a marble spec beside its module. | A critic or reviewer shows that one of the nine has a stream transformation at its core, or a reading marked illustrative claims to run. |
